## Plain-language summary (read this first)

**What S148 is.** In S147 the owner decided how the state's agreements should be read (ADR-043): the norm is a calendar month, every hour above it is flex, and only hours a leader ordered become overtime. S148 does not yet change how anyone is paid. It does two things first. **Stage 0** removes the places where today's system pays or approves something it should not. **The config stage** makes the configuration editors' values the values payroll uses: each one dated, resolved for every day, and frozen into the month's proof, instead of payroll quietly using rules compiled into the code. The test that holds the sprint honest is easy to say: every demo payslip comes out the same before and after. The monthly norm, where payslips do change, is S149.

**The order it lands in, and why.**
0. **The pay pin, first and alone (wave 0).** Before anything changes, a test builds the two demo worlds, runs payroll on every month the demo approves, and stores the result as a fixed "golden" file. Docker does not run on the owner's machine, so the golden is captured by CI from code identical to the base. Every later checkpoint must reproduce it byte for byte.
1. **Stage 0's independent fixes (wave 1).** Payroll's "the same person signed twice" refusal gets its own name and comes before calculating; the old time-registration route goes, and payroll refuses any stored hour typed as something other than ordinary work; a day can no longer hold more paid absence than it was worth; overtime compensation refuses when its own guarded update refused; the orphan list stops counting a departed manager as cover; the editors say "does not yet affect pay"; two small pin sets.
2. **Skema's save path (wave 2).** The leftover "manual hours" retire; children's sick days follow the agreement texts per OK version, per illness, with the call-home day. This is the sprint's one change to pay arithmetic (a new credited code), so the evaluation marker moves once, to `weekly-timeline-v3`.
3. **The rest check (wave 3).** The 11-hour check reads the clock intervals employees type in Skema; the screen's instant warning and the server's check are proved to agree.
   **3b. The corrected rest check (wave 3b, owner Q2, "final + 10").** Verified research showed that wave 3 measures rest the wrong way: it compares one working day with the next, so a 24-hour shift, a day shift plus a night shift, or a split day over 13 hours looks compliant. The authority's test is to look 24 hours back from the end of every piece of work: the longest unbroken rest in that stretch must be at least 11 hours. Wave 3b rebuilds that one calculation on the server and on the screen, in real Danish time (the daylight-saving nights are 23 and 25 hours), dates a finding on the day the work ends, and stops the "derogation allowed" setting from excusing rests under 8 hours. Some numbers and dates the screens show change on purpose (a 6,0 becomes an 8,0 dated a day later); no pay figure changes. It merges before wave 5 reopens the same rule.
4. **The config stage's foundation (wave 4).** The schema (dated configs, the local profile's new settings, customary days, the HR facts, the dated organisation); two retirements; dated config reads; the dated organisation and the dated transfer; and the "pay inputs" lock on every Backend writer that dates a pay input into the past.
5. **Payroll joins (wave 5).** Transfers split a month in payroll; a past-dated publish lands the paid months it reaches on HR's worklist; one dated, fail-closed lookup; **the engine computes only with the configs it is handed (the moment the editors reach pay)**; configs frozen by value into each month's proof; and payroll's half of the lock, which refuses "calculate again" when an input changed during the recording.
6. **The editors (wave 6).** The local profile's new settings, customary days, the HR facts editor, the date field on the central editors.
7. **The demo (wave 7).** The demo world gains profiles, customs and HR facts with no pay effect; the pay pin runs a final time.

**Why the pay path moves in one place.** Today the screens and payroll read configuration differently, and payroll ignores the database entirely. If payroll were switched over piece by piece, there would be a moment when a payslip used one rule and the screen another. So every step before wave 5's engine switch (T14b) is invisible to pay, the switch is one merge, and the lock's payroll half (T24) follows it inside the same wave, so no checkpoint ever sees payroll reading dated configs without its "calculate again" check (`REF:786`).

**What this plan adds to the refinement** (each is a placement the refinement left to the plan, a gap found while drafting, or a correction from the plan review; none changes the scope, the business intent or an owner ruling):
- the five NOTEs the refinement's last review carried here, placed task by task (N-1 … N-5);
- task ids, waves, stacks, checkpoints and test names; T14b delivered as a stack of three specialist implementers and two test passes merged as one (declared deviation DV-2): the first test pass comes straight after the engine half, so the payroll and Backend specialists always work on a test tree that compiles (NR-P9);
- three test areas the refinement's churn census did not name: the composed-stack smoke suite posts to the retiring route and calls the engine without a config (it runs only in CI); the test-side schema copies and SQL inserts of every table T11 changes (the S144 post-close defect class); and the smoke probe the standing "every changed hop gets a composed probe" principle requires for `check-overtime-governance`;
- the audit-projection catalog rows, which live under `docs/` and so are written by the Orchestrator in the same merge as each new event;
- CI evidence runs for twenty-two Docker mutations over eighteen AC ids (AC-C2p carries two, M-15 and M-16; AC-S0-2e three, M-8, M-22 and M-23; AC-C2a two, M-39 and M-40), each showing a Docker-gated pin's RED (six in drafts 1–1.2; M-14 and M-15 added in 1.3, M-16 in 1.4.1, M-22 and M-23 in "final + 6", M-39 and M-40 in "final + 13", M-41 and M-42 in "final + 14", M-43 in "final + 17", M-44 and M-45 in "final + 18", M-46 and M-47 in "final + 19", M-48 in "final + 24", M-49 in "final + 25"; addendum A1's M-17 … M-21 are counted in the addendum);
- the pay-inputs lock's key, named: `(148, 1)`;
- **from the first plan review (Step 0b cycle 1, draft 1.3), six decisions the refinement left open or got slightly wrong:**
  - a person created in the product gets their first organisation row from the start of history (`0001-01-01`), not from the day they were created, so HR can still backdate a new person's first working day without leaving days that have a profile but no organisation, which payroll would refuse (NR-P7; the one place the plan departs from the refinement's text, DV-7);
  - an edited position override keeps its old version in a history table beside the editable row, so recalculating an old month finds the override as it was then (NR-P8);
  - two publishes dated the same day resolve the same way every time: the later one is in force (NR-P11);
  - a start date set from "unknown" to a date still flags the settled holiday years it reaches, even when no month has been paid yet (NR-P12);
  - two small screen fixes: the profile editor's two overtime fields keep a "does not yet affect pay" note when the general label goes, because it stays true for them (NR-P13), and when the server refuses a mid-week date the central editors offer the nearest valid Monday (NR-P14);
  - the task that retires config fingerprints owns every line that still names them (NR-P10).
- **from the second plan review (Step 0b cycle 2, draft 1.4):** a publish is stamped at the moment it takes effect, not at the moment its database transaction began, so "the later publish wins" stays true when two administrators publish at nearly the same time; otherwise the one that started first but finished second could win on its older stamp, and payroll would use a version the editor shows as archived (NR-P16); and the central editors offer a Monday only when the server would accept it (on or after the newest published version's date), otherwise they say the earliest possible date is next Monday, so a correction the editor suggests is never refused again (NR-P17). The other five rulings are mechanics: where four engine tests are proved red (NR-P18), which task may edit the last lines naming a retired item (NR-P19), the date a holiday-year hand-off shows when the year's window cannot be placed (NR-P20), that every override writer records its dated version itself (NR-P21), and two test-setup conditions (NR-P22).
- **from the third plan review (Step 0b cycle 3, draft 1.5):** a mutation run now continues to the database tests after an expected unit-test failure, and its expected-red list is everything the mutation breaks; two test-database copies gain the override-history table. *Why:* in CI the unit tests run first, and a red unit test skipped the database tests the run existed to observe; and a list naming only the pin would read every other expected red as a false finding. The evidence branches carry a three-line workflow change that never reaches master (NR-P25 … NR-P27). The two copies are hand-written test databases whose tests save overrides, which from wave 4 always write a history row (NR-P28).

**How it is checked.** Every acceptance criterion names its test, its kind and one owning task. **F** fails on today's code; **N** names something the sprint creates and proves RED by one named mutation (locally at the wave gate for a Unit pin, in a CI evidence run for a Docker pin); **R** a regression pin that holds today and must keep holding. A Docker fact is "CI-pending" until a checkpoint run shows it green.

---

## How the waves are built — read before the ledger

1. **The pin first and alone.** TASK-14800 is wave 0. Its production code equals the base, so CI captures PIN-1's golden from it (CI-W0), the Orchestrator commits the captured text verbatim, and CI-W0b confirms the pin green against it. Wave-1 agents may be dispatched once TASK-14800 merges; **no wave-1 merge before CI-W0b is green**.
2. **Foundations land first in their wave's merge order and are seen green in CI before anything builds on them.** T11's schema and the dated config reads (CI-W4a, after TASK-14815 → 14816 → 14818); the Backend half of the lock and the dated organisation (CI-W4, before wave 5); the payroll organisation boundary, the publish hand-off and the dated lookup (CI-W5a, before the engine switch).
3. **File overlap is designed out by order, not by merge luck.** The table below lists every production file two tasks touch; each pair is in different waves or merges in a stated order, and each task touches only the lines named for it.
4. **Contract-changing backend tasks are stacked with their `ux` follow-up** (S146/S147's pattern): REGEN regenerates `docs/api/openapi.json` on the backend branch, the `ux` task branches from it and runs `npm run gen:api`, and the stack merges together, so master's `tsc` is never red between two merges.
5. **Worktrees.** Every implementer, stacked `ux` task and test-only task runs with `isolation: "worktree"`, branched from LOCAL master (the freshness instruction is the first standing constraint). A paired `-T` pass works on its implementer's branch after the implementer returns. No `git stash` anywhere.
6. **Inside a wave the later-merging task merges master before its gate** (rebuild, re-run its suites); a test file two `-T` passes in one wave both report is resolved by that rebuild, never by hand-merging assertions.
7. **The pay path's two halves stay inside one wave**: T14b (the engine switch) and T24 (payroll's recheck under the lock) both merge in wave 5, and no checkpoint runs between them (`REF:786`). CI-W5a runs before T14b; CI-W5 after T24.
8. **The PIL block.** Every brief whose task writes, edits or tests a transaction that takes the pay-inputs lock carries the PIL standing block (below) verbatim: TASK-14820, 14821, 14823, 14825, 14831, 14832 and their paired passes. TASK-14834 and 14836 carry its one-line negative ("does not take the lock in S148"). TASK-14845 and 14845-T carry it too, though none of their 22 sites takes the lock: its lock orders are what their new locked reads must not invert, and its Skema-save line is TASK-14845's ("final + 19").

## The merge checklist — every task, paired pass, stack and fix-up

1. **Report read.** Brief contradictions, declared deviations and the "Not built / not checked", "Found, not fixed" and "Shortcuts taken" sections are dispositioned: factual ones by the Orchestrator; a requirement, invariant or ruling question to the `adjudicator` (Fable, or the labelled stand-in).
2. **Build.** `dotnet build` 0 errors; warnings ≤ 144; CA2100 ≤ 114 (CI ratchet 115). A new test-project CA2100 gets the QUAL-073 justified suppression, as S147 did in `2e445df8`.
3. **Step 5α — `constraint-validator`** on `git diff <base>...<branch>`, scope = the task's authorized files (a paired branch or a stack: the union; any `src/**` change in a `-T` pass's own commits is a violation).
4. **Security consult** (TASK-14820, 14821, **14845** ("final + 19"), 14834, 14836; **and the TASK-14813 fix-up 1 diff, the two manager-visibility reads** ("final + 8", 14813 Step 5a adjudication, Orchestrator item); the refinement's "security consult"). A `security` spawn (Sonnet), told it is read-only, reads the brief and the diff and returns the negative-test list and any residual it would register. Saved as `.claude/reviews/S148-TASK-<n>-security-consult.md` before the Orchestrator acts on it; input to the 5a reviewer, not a lens.
5. **Step 5a — internal lens** (`reviewer` on Fable 5.2; while Fable is limited, the labelled Opus stand-in) on the branch diff with the pasted brief. **Saved as `.claude/reviews/S148-TASK-<n>-5a-reviewer.md` BEFORE acting on it** (`docs/WORKFLOW.md:32`). Concrete-case rule: a BLOCKER or WARNING names its case; anything else is an `(unproven)` NOTE with its evidence, recorded with a disposition.
6. **Step 5a — Codex** on the rows marked high-risk (`docs/AGENTS.md:651-661`), **run inside the task's worktree with the commit range named in the prompt** (S146/S147 practice): `codex exec "<prompt naming <base>..<branch-tip>>" < /dev/null --output-last-message <file>`; saved as `.claude/reviews/S148-TASK-<n>-5a-codex.md` (+ `.log`) before acting. If its sandbox blocks its shell, the inlined-source fallback, and the verdict says so.
7. **Merge**, then GATE on master; the sprint-log row `task · branch · build · 5α · consult · 5a-R · 5a-C · merged sha` and the handoff row are written. **An empty 5α or 5a cell blocks the merge.** Cap: 5 cycles per lens per task; halt-and-prompt after cycle 5 only if it finds new BLOCKERs.
8. **Shortcuts (PAT-032).** Each "Shortcuts taken" line is registered (a QUAL, SEC or F row, a dated note on a row, or a `QUAL-BF` row) and its `shortcut(pending)` replaced by the tag **in the same merge** (`docs/WORKFLOW.md:35`); check the code's `shortcut` comments against the list both ways.
9. **Same-merge docs** (the Orchestrator): the audit-projection catalog rows for every new event a task emits (O-items; `AuditProjectionParityTests` reads the catalog at test time, `tests/StatsTid.Tests.Regression/PhaseE/AuditProjectionParityTests.cs:12-38`); `docs/generated/db-schema.md` hand-synced after a schema merge (Python is not installed locally; CI's `check_docs.py --check` arbitrates; the S144–S146 precedent, `git log -- docs/generated/db-schema.md`).

## Standing constraints — pasted verbatim into every agent prompt

**Block A — every agent.**
```
★ FIRST, BEFORE ANY WORK: check your worktree against master.
Run `git log --oneline -1` and compare with the main checkout's LOCAL master — not `origin/master`,
which lags by design because this project pushes only at sprint close. Fast-forward if you are
behind, and say in your report what you found.
```
```
Do NOT use `git stash` — it is repository-global, not per-worktree, and will collide with
other agents running concurrently. To prove a RED by reverting a production change:
    git diff > <scratch>/mychange.patch
    git checkout -- <the production files>
    ... run the test, capture the failure output ...
    git apply <scratch>/mychange.patch

RED-FIRST (docs/AGENTS.md § "RED evidence"). Every new or changed fact says in its docstring which kind
it is, and its "Red conditions":
 F  an assertion that FAILS ON TODAY'S CODE. Name that assertion under "Red conditions". Unit: run it
    on master before the implementation is merged and quote the failure. Docker: you cannot run it
    locally; name the production behaviour that makes that assertion fail today. If no assertion
    of the fact fails today, it is NOT F: label it R and give it a discriminating assertion.
 N  names a type or member this sprint creates. Prove RED by ONE named mutation (the plan's M-list),
    never by reverting. Unit: at your gate, `git worktree add --detach <path outside the repo> master`
    (a pass inside an unmerged stack: the SHA the plan's mutation row names),
    apply the mutation (keep the API, break the one behaviour), run the fact, capture the assertion
    failure, `git -C <path> checkout -- .`, then `git worktree remove --force <path>` and
    `git worktree prune` (never cp/Copy-Item a backup back: FAIL-005). A compile error never counts.
    Docker: argue it in the docstring, and name in your report every existing fact, in any suite, you
    expect the mutation to turn red; the Orchestrator runs the mutation in a CI evidence run.
 R  a regression pin (an existing behaviour kept). Label it so.
TEST NAMES ARE CONTRACT where a brief gives them; if you must rename, report old → new.

CENSUS RULE. Where a brief changes a constructor, a signature, a vocabulary, a table or a route, it states
the census COMMAND (`git grep`, so bin/obj never match) and you classify EVERY hit as (a) authorized
for edit (named, with what may change), (b) expected and unchanged (named, one-line reason), or
(c) generated (named; regenerated by REGEN, never hand-edited). Re-run the command and report the
true counts. An unclassified hit is a STOP and report, never an edit.

GREP GATES. Every "grep is empty" gate runs over `src tests frontend/src tools` (and
`docker/postgres/init.sql` where named) only. Hits under docs/**, .claude/** and the sprint logs are
expected history.

DATES. Test dates are the ones the brief names (each weekday was checked); "today" in a fact comes from
the injected TimeProvider through CopenhagenBusinessDate.Today. Absences fall on WEEKDAYS unless a fact
is about a weekend.
```
- **`docs/CONVENTIONS.md` verbatim** — the Orchestrator pastes it into every prompt.
- **KB entries:** the ledger's "KB pasted" column, pasted whole.
- **Docker is unavailable locally.** Report Docker facts as **"CI-pending"**; the sprint log flips a fact to "CI-verified" only with the checkpoint run id that showed it green. The local gate is build + Unit + DemoSeed + Regression `Category!=Docker` (+ the five frontend checks for frontend work), in the foreground, `-m:2`. **The composed-stack smoke suite (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Smoke`) and the e2e suite run only in CI**; a brief that touches a route or hop either suite exercises names it.
- **Python is not installed locally:** `tools/check_docs.py`, `tools/check_openapi_*.py` and `tools/generate_db_schema.py` are CI-only.
- **Read exit status from the unpiped command.**
- **Never edit a generated file by hand:** `docs/api/openapi.json`, `frontend/src/lib/api-types.ts`, `docs/generated/db-schema.md`. **No agent edits `docs/**`** (Orchestrator-only) — including `docs/operations/audit-projection-catalog.md`: report each new event's catalog row (event, scope, org resolution, subject, payload keys, mapper kind) and the Orchestrator writes it in the same merge.
- **No employment date in any employee-facing response body, error body or DTO** (ADR-040 D7). Every 403 this sprint adds carries a date-free reason; the server log names the clause.
- **"Today" is read once per handler**, from the injected `TimeProvider` through `CopenhagenBusinessDate.Today` (PAT-028), and passed down.
- **Reseed, don't migrate.** A schema change is an `init.sql` edit; no upgrade path, no runbook row.
- **Report anything the brief does not name** (an enumeration site, a caller, a test, a harness copy, a schema copy).
- **Contract vs. hint.** A "*Hint (HOW)*" is one way; the contract is the WHAT. Deviate from a hint freely and say so; from a contract only by reporting.

**Block B — every implementer and `ux` brief, verbatim from `docs/AGENTS.md:328-364`:** the SMALLEST COMPLETE CHANGE block (identity `f57be923033c5ab9`, `SPRINT-148.md:13`) and the BRIEF CHALLENGE block. Each brief then names, from the per-task notes below, its **never-extras** (the machinery the block must not skip), its **"Not built"** list (`REF:831`) and its **likely `shortcut(pending)` limits**.

**Block C — the PIL block, every brief that writes, edits or tests a transaction taking the pay-inputs lock** (TASK-14820, 14821, 14823, 14825, 14831, 14832 and their paired passes; also TASK-14845 and 14845-T for its lock orders, `:70`, "final + 19"):
```
PAY-INPUTS LOCK (S148 PIL; refinement A-3, B-2, B-7, B-8). One transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory
lock, one key for the whole system, in the TWO-INTEGER key space: (148, 1). No two-integer advisory key
exists in src/ today, and PostgreSQL keeps that space apart from the single-key space every existing
lock (hashtext 'employee-', 'payroll-ledger-', 'reporting-org-', 'unit-org-', 'position-override-') uses. In pg_locks it shows
as locktype 'advisory', classid 148, objid 1, objsubid 2. Taken only through PayInputsLock
(src/Infrastructure/StatsTid.Infrastructure/PayInputsLock.cs, TASK-14821):
  EXCLUSIVE, as the FIRST statement of the transaction, in every writer that dates a pay input into
  the past or changes one from a day a recording may still cover;
  SHARED, as the FIRST statement of the transaction, in the payroll export and the correction.
Every transaction that takes it runs at READ COMMITTED, stated explicitly.
THE DEADLOCK RULE, PER REQUEST:
 (1) a request waiting for the pay-inputs lock holds no lock on ANY connection — nothing is taken
     before it, on this connection or another;
 (2) the lock is taken only at a route's own transaction head (or a service's that owns its
     transaction), never inside a repository helper or a shared writer;
 (3) a read on a separate connection never takes it, not even shared (the profile route's resolver
     read on its own connection, EmployeeProfileEndpoints.cs:1780, :1785-1786, would otherwise wait
     for its own request forever).
LOCK ORDER: the pay-inputs lock first; no transaction takes it after any other lock.
  Export: pay inputs (shared) → payroll-ledger- → the rechecks (reads) → previous month's approval
    FOR SHARE → this month's approval FOR UPDATE → the record.
  Correction: pay inputs (shared) → payroll-ledger- → the rechecks → the record FOR UPDATE.
  User edit: pay inputs (exclusive, when the request carries an organisation or an agreement code) →
    reporting-org- (both roots, id order) → unit-org- (both organisations, id order) → the users row
    FOR UPDATE → the agreement-code and organisation rows → the worklist rows. A drift retry rolls
    back and takes it first again.
  Dedicated agreement-code route: pay inputs → the users row FOR UPDATE → the agreement-code rows →
    the worklist rows.
  Employee-profile route: pay inputs → employee- → the profile rows → the revaluation → the worklist rows.
  Profile close: pay inputs → the profile timeline rows FOR UPDATE → the users row FOR UPDATE → the
    close and the retired scheduled rows → the audit rows and events → the worklist rows.
  Employment start-date PUT: pay inputs → employee- → the users row FOR UPDATE → the guards → the
    write → the users_audit row and the start-date event → the worklist rows.
  Employment end-date PUT: pay inputs → employee- → the users row FOR UPDATE → the guards → the
    lifecycle writer → the worklist rows.
  Settlement reversal, every mode: pay inputs (when it carries an end-date correction) → employee- →
    the users row FOR UPDATE → the settlement rows; with an end-date correction only, then the span
    and strand guards → the lifecycle writer → the worklist rows; in reverse-and-supersede mode, then
    the re-settlement.
  Agreement-config publish, local-profile write: pay inputs → the config or profile row FOR UPDATE →
    the worklist rows.
  Override writes: pay inputs → the position-override- key lock → the override rows FOR UPDATE →
    the version row → the worklist rows. The position-override- key lock (single-key space, one per
    agreement, OK version and position) is taken in the repository, ahead of the row lock; rule (2)
    is about the pay-inputs lock only. Only override writers take it, and a transaction holding
    position-override- takes no other advisory lock.
  Skema save on another's behalf: employee- → the users row FOR UPDATE.
NOT TAKEN by the user create, the customary-days writers or the HR-facts writers in S148.
```

**Block D — every paired `-T` pass:** you are on another agent's branch. Edit only the listed files and **never `src/**`**; a production defect is reported, not fixed. Change an expected value only where the brief lists it as intentional, labelled `// S148 <ruling>:`; any other value that changes is a **STOP and report**. **Never re-pin PIN-1's golden** (only the Orchestrator commits it, once, at O-1).

**both stamps are taken after serialisation, a requirement (NR-P16):** the activating UPDATE stamps `published_at = clock_timestamp()`, not today's `NOW()` (`AgreementConfigRepository.cs:449`), because `NOW()` is the transaction's start and the publish runs at the default READ COMMITTED (`AgreementConfigEndpoints.cs:479` opens it with no isolation level), so a publish whose transaction began first but activates second would carry the older stamp, archive the newer version and still lose the tie-break to it; **position overrides as dated versions (NR-P8):** each of the four routes (create `PositionOverrideEndpoints.cs:80`, edit `:182`, deactivate `:340`, activate `:448`) takes the nullable date member and resolves an omitted date to today; its audit `new_data` carries the date; the two self-managed writers with no caller under `src` or `tools`, `CreateAsync(entity)` `:70` and `DeactivateAsync(Guid)` `:273-298` (the create route uses `CreateReturningAsync`, `PositionOverrideEndpoints.cs:130`), are deleted; the in-force read is by key (agreement, OK version, position) across all override ids of that key: the newest version row on or before the day, ordered by `applies_from` then `recorded_at`, both descending; ACTIVE gives its values, INACTIVE or no row gives no override; a new date may not precede the key's newest version date, and on an equal date the later write wins; **the predecessor rule holds for any number of concurrent writers of one key (B4):** the write's first lock takes every row of the key `FOR UPDATE` in id order (configs by agreement and OK version; override heads by agreement, OK version and position), then reads the predecessor; **an override write takes, before those rows, the key's transaction-scoped advisory lock, as its own statement, the first of `LockKeyAsync` (`PositionOverrideRepository.cs:602` at `af4325ba`), so all four writers take it: `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('position-override-' || @agreementCode || '|' || @okVersion || '|' || @positionCode))`; a key that has never had an override has no row to lock, and without it three writers could store a version dated before versions already committed (the TASK-14818 Step 5a cycle-1 adjudication, ruling 1.1, "final + 17"; Block C's override-write order; the agreement-config publish needs no advisory lock, its own draft row being a committed row of the key)**; a refusal is 400 with the contract code `BEFORE_PREDECESSOR` (AC-C2q); ruling 1.1)**; the Monday rule applies to any of the four writes that changes the in-force value of a week-read field, deactivation and activation included; `PositionOverrideEffectiveDates` (`PeriodCalculationService.cs:1970`) reads the version dates; ## Declared deviations from the refinement's work breakdown (DV-1 … DV-6, DV-8) and its text (DV-7) (for Step 0b to confirm; DV-8 added by ruling, "final + 13")

- **DV-1 — T13 (C-6, C-7) goes to `backend-infrastructure`, not `sweep`.** The refinement offered either (`REF:812`). The retirement spans entities, repositories' SQL, three events, the seed and four editors: about 30 sites of different kinds, which is judgment work, not one pattern (the S143 TASK-14306a lesson, `docs/operations/model-routing-register.md:123`).
- **DV-2 — T14b is delivered as a stack: `rule-engine` (14826) → `test-qa` (14826-T) → `payroll-integration` (14827) → `backend-infrastructure` (14828) → `test-qa` (14828-T) → REGEN → `ux` (14829), merged together as one merge.** Scope unchanged; the refinement's "one merge" (the engine refusing a configless request breaks every caller, so all callers change in the same merge, `REF:409`, `:414`) is kept, while each domain gets its specialist. **As amended by NR-P9 (draft 1.3):** the test work is two passes, not one. 14826 deletes `AgreementConfigProvider`, which nine test files name, so 14826-T comes straight after it on its branch: it re-points those files, writes the shared request helper and the engine facts, and its gate is both test projects compiling and Unit green, with the Regression facts expected red until the callers land listed in its report. 14827 and 14828 must leave both test projects compiling (a signature change that would break them is a stop and report, and the Orchestrator dispatches a compile repair to `test-qa` before the next step). 14828-T at the tip writes everything else. The full local GATE still runs only at the stack tip (until 14827 and 14828 land, the callers' Regression facts fail by design). **As amended by NR-P18 (draft 1.4):** 14826-T's four Unit mutations (M-2 … M-5) are run at its gate in a worktree detached at its recorded branch-tip SHA, not at master, because the stack reaches master only at its tip; the merge order is unchanged. *Given up:* one more dispatch. *Alternative:* one `payroll-integration` agent with a cross-domain label over the engine, the Backend and the Orchestrator, as S147's TASK-14703 did; rejected because the task is the sprint's largest and riskiest, and one agent holding four domains is where S141–S142's brief contradictions concentrated. NR-P1.
- **DV-3 — T20 (C-12) goes to `backend-infrastructure` extended into `tools/StatsTid.DemoSeed/**`, with a `test-qa` paired pass**, not to `test-qa` alone (`REF:827`). The generator's new rows are production tooling logic with a hard "no RNG draw" invariant behind PIN-1; S147's TASK-14704 carried the demo loader the same way.
- **DV-4 — T11 creates every new event shape, C-13's included; T21a emits.** The refinement's T21a row lists "data-model (the transfer event)" (`REF:815`) while T11's row "owns … the entity, model and event shapes" (`REF:811`) and merges first. One owner per file region (BC-P4).
- **DV-5 — every contract-changing backend task is split into a backend task and a stacked `ux` task** (14803, 14810, 14812, 14814, 14817, 14824, 14829, 14833, 14835, 14837), where the refinement listed `ux` as a role inside the task. Scope unchanged; S146/S147's stack pattern keeps master's `tsc` green between merges.
- **DV-6 — test ownership follows what each fact needs to run, not the refinement's task rows, in four places** (BC-P2, BC-P3): AC-C13a's pay half, AC-C2a's and AC-C2c's pay halves and AC-C2m's pay half are written in TASK-14828-T (14826-T until NR-P9 split the stack's test pass, draft 1.3), because payroll reads dated configs only from T14b (`REF:414`); AC-C13b, -c and -g are T22's (payroll-side), not T21a's.
- **DV-7 — a product-created person's first organisation row runs from `0001-01-01`, not from the day of the create (NR-P7, draft 1.3).** *In plain words:* the refinement had the user create write the person's first organisation row from the create's date (`REF:543`), and its AC-C13d expects a person created and transferred the same day to end with one row, from today (`REF:560`). That breaks a feature S138 shipped: HR may correct a new person's profile and agreement code back to their real first day, before the first row (`EmployeeProfileRepository.cs:679`, "a date before the first row gets a row ending where the first begins"; `UserAgreementCodeRepository.cs:542`, `InsertBeforeFirst`), while the create dates its rows today (`AdminEndpoints.cs:1172-1188`) and no route can move an organisation row earlier. The days between the real first day and the create would then have a profile but no organisation, and C-13's fail-loud resolver (`REF:549`) would refuse them in payroll and on the screens. *The change:* the create's first organisation row starts at `0001-01-01`, the history-covering anchor the backfill seeder uses (`UserAgreementCodeBackfillSeeder.cs:14`, `:28-30`), so every day a profile can ever cover has an organisation. AC-C13d's same-day case then ends with two rows, the creating organisation [`0001-01-01`, today) and the new one from today; the in-place shape stays for a second transfer on one date; the "before the live row" 400 needs a prior dated transfer to set it up. AC-C13k pins the backdating case. *Given up:* the organisation table no longer says when someone joined their first institution; the employment start date already does. *Rejected:* reading the earliest row as open towards the past, which would put a special case in the resolver, 14823's matching intervals, payroll's plan histories and the lock's recheck. The refinement is not edited (rev 5.1 READY is the record).
- **DV-8 — AC-C2a's split fact reads the planner directly; the payroll host gains the two dated sources at TASK-14825 (wave-4 brief adjudication B2, "final + 13").** *In plain words:* the refinement wires payroll's planner to the dated configuration tables in T12 (`REF:813`, "payroll-integration (boundary sources `PeriodCalculationService.cs:1969-1970`)"), and its AC-C2a is one Docker F fact. But `PeriodCalculationService` holds neither config repository (its constructor `:304-319` at `e9d09a66`) and the payroll host registers neither. TASK-14818 therefore adds the hydration behind two optional, null-tolerant constructor dependencies (S144's planner-source shape), and TASK-14825, which already registers what the lookup needs (QUAL-184), registers the two repositories and so switches the sources on. Until then production payroll neither splits nor prices on a dated version. AC-C2a's split fact builds the planner directly with the new dependencies, which makes it N, proved by M-39 (the hydration skipped) at CI-M4a. *Given up:* a host-level proof of the split in wave 4. *Rejected:* registering the repositories in wave 4 (TASK-14818 BC-2 (b)), which would have production payroll split months while still pricing on compiled rules: a change to pay arithmetic with no test over the host, against "every step before wave 5's engine switch (T14b) is invisible to pay" (`PLAN:38`). The refinement is not edited. **Interim, told not asked ("final + 25"; the S148 wave-5 batch-A/B brief adjudication, B9, as ruled in B2 of wave 4):** from TASK-14825's merge until the T14b stack's, production payroll splits a month at a past-dated published version but prices both parts on compiled rules (this refines O-12's item (1): from TASK-14825 payroll splits on a past-dated publish; from the T14b stack it prices on it). No invariant is traded: every seeded version applies from `0001-01-01` and the demo loader calls none of the six configuration-writing routes, so PIN-1 sees no split (CI-W5a's watch item "PIN-1 green with the hydration on"); the window is local master between two merges of one wave and no export record survives it (reseed), so a month exported there is priced on the built-in rules it is priced on today; that a split alone moves no amount stays `(unproven)` until AC-C2a's pay half at CI-W5.

## The five carried NOTEs, and where each landed

*From `.claude/reviews/REFINEMENT-s148-reviewer-c5.md:9-13` (an Opus stand-in, cycle 5).*

**N-1 — an unchanged end date re-saved lists nothing; name the `EffectiveFrom` of an interval open towards the past.** *Verified:* the lifecycle writer writes and emits whether or not the date changed (`src/Infrastructure/StatsTid.Infrastructure/EmploymentEndDateLifecycleWriter.cs:153-220`; the end-date PUT reaches it with no no-op test before it, `EmploymentDateEndpoints.cs:623`), and the end date's interval [x, x + 1 day) is never empty, while the worklist writer skips only an empty interval (`HrBackdateWorklistRepository.cs:889`). **Landed in TASK-14821:** none of the three employment-date writers calls the worklist writer when the old and new date are equal (both null included). **The open-towards-the-past case:** a start date set from none to a date, or cleared to none, changes every day before the dated one (`init.sql:2977`: the start is nullable). The worklist writer needs a concrete `from` (`HrBackdateWorklistRepository.cs:879-886`) and every trigger's `EffectiveFrom` means "the earliest day whose change can alter the flagged period" (`:39-56`). **Proposal (NR-P2):** for such an interval, `from` and `EffectiveFrom` are **the Monday of the opening week of the employee's earliest exported month** (that month's first settled day, read inside the writer's transaction after the lock — the same meaning `PRIOR_MONTH_REOPENED` uses, `:51-56`); with no exported month, no call. *Rejected:* `0001-01-01` (a date HR would read as meaningless). **Amended by NR-P12 (draft 1.3): that start and its "no exported month, no call" govern the exported-month call only.** *Why:* settled holiday years are selected independently of exports (`WriteForSettledYearsAsync`, `HrBackdateWorklistRepository.cs:1027-1057`), and the refinement requires both calls (`REF:613`), so "no call" would leave a settled holiday year unflagged for an employee who has no paid month yet, and a new start before the earliest exported month would give the settled-year call an empty interval (`:1037-1038`). **The settled-year call is always made when old ≠ new:** `from` = `DateOnly.MinValue`, `toExclusive` = the dated side, and the existing two-part rule (`ThreatensSettledYearAsync`, `:1473-1503`: the valuation boundary, then the entitlement window) picks exactly the threatened years. **The settled-year row's `EffectiveFrom`** is the earliest "look from" day among the selected settlements, the field's own meaning ("the earliest day whose change can alter the flagged period", `:39-56`), so it never shows `0001-01-01`; it needs one small repository overload in TASK-14821. **Completed by NR-P20 (draft 1.4):** a settlement's "look from" day is its accrual window's first day, or 1 January of its entitlement year when the window cannot be placed. *Why:* the selection keeps, conservatively, a settlement whose entitlement type has no live reset month for the employee's agreement and OK version, without placing its window (`HrBackdateWorklistRepository.cs:1495-1496`), so "the window's first day" alone would leave the implementer to throw or write `0001-01-01`; 1 January is a true lower bound, because every accrual window starts in its entitlement year (`src/SharedKernel/StatsTid.SharedKernel/Calendar/EntitlementPeriodResolver.cs:151`, `:171`, `:176`). *Given up:* in that rare branch the date can be up to eleven months earlier than the real window start; conservative, never meaningless. **AC rows added to AC-PIL-h:** **(7)** an end date re-saved unchanged (Tuesday 30 June 2026 → Tuesday 30 June 2026, June 2026 exported) answers 200 and lands no worklist row; **(8)** a start date set from none to Monday 15 June 2026, with June 2026 exported and registrations only from the 15th, lists June with `EffectiveFrom` Monday 1 June 2026 (June opens on a Monday); **(9) (NR-P12)** with an ACTIVE settlement and no exported month, a start set from none to a date inside or after that holiday year, nothing stranded: one SETTLED_YEAR row with the employment-date trigger and the start-date event, no EXPORTED_MONTH row, nothing revalued; **(10) (NR-P12)** a new start before the earliest exported month's opening Monday, with such a settlement: the SETTLED_YEAR row lands and no EXPORTED_MONTH row; a settlement whose window begins on or after the new start is not flagged; **(11) (NR-P20)** an ACTIVE settlement whose entitlement type has no live reset month for the employee's agreement and OK version, the start set from none to a date: the PUT answers 200 and one SETTLED_YEAR row carries `EffectiveFrom` = 1 January of that entitlement year. All five in TASK-14821-T.

**N-2 — the screen formula's rule when no record covers the month end.** *Verified:* today Teamoversigt takes the period's agreement, else the users row's, with the users row's OK version (`ApprovalEndpoints.cs:1346`, `:1374`, `:1381-1386`), and fraction 1.0 when no profile covers the month end or the read fails (`:1318-1339`, `:1389`); the balance takes the agreement dated at month start, else the live one (`BalanceEndpoints.cs:120-121`), the month's OK version (`:159`), and fraction 1.0 with no dated profile, tolerating the resolver's exception (`:136-152`); neither reads a position or an organisation for such an employee. **Landed in TASK-14828 (the rule) and TASK-14828-T (the pin; 14826-T until NR-P9):** on a day no record covers, when the month end has no record either (a profile closed Friday 20 March 2026; an employee with no profile at all), W_d is the agreement config's weekly norm in force on day d for today's agreement source (Teamoversigt: the period's, else the users row's; the balance: dated at month start, else the live one), **central only** (no position override, no local profile), at **day d's OK version** (NR-P6; BC-P10), and f = 1.0 (ADR-023 D3). **No record is a graceful absence, not a read failure:** the screen keeps rendering; C-1's fail-closed rule applies to a failed read. **The R pin, AC-C1n (4):** for March 2026 on a 37-hour agreement, an employee whose profile closes Friday 20 March 2026 and an employee with no profile both get 200 and **162.80 h** (22 × 7.40) on Teamoversigt and on the balance, today's figure. **Amended by the T14b stack's 5a ruling T2 (2026-11-23, `.claude/reviews/S148-T14b-5a-adjudication-c1.md`):** on a weekday no dated record covers, when the key asked has no configuration in force that day (a new agreement published from a later date), the day reads the same key's configuration in force at month end, and counts 0.00 h if none is in force at month end either. It is the second ruled exception to B4's no-catch rule: exactly `NoConfigInForceException`, exactly on a no-record day, inside `ComputeMonthNormAsync` only. A record-covered day and a failed read still refuse.

**N-3 `(unproven)` — summing day by day may slow Teamoversigt.** *Verified:* the per-day calculator calls the profile resolver once per weekday per employee (`DailyNormCalculator.cs:92-99`), where today's Teamoversigt resolves one weekly norm per distinct (agreement, OK version) pair (`ApprovalEndpoints.cs:1354-1365`). **Landed in TASK-14828 (the cause) and TASK-14825-T / TASK-14828-T (the evidence; 14826-T until NR-P9):** the stack resolves W_d **once per stretch between dated-record changes** (each employee's dated records read once for the month; one lookup per stretch where the agreement, OK version, position, organisation and every applies-from boundary are constant). The timing fact is written by TASK-14825-T so that **CI-W5a records the "before" figure** on the code just before T14b, and TASK-14828-T re-runs it unchanged so **CI-W5 records the "after" figure** — the one placement that gives a true before/after in CI. The comparison and the NOTE's disposition are T14b's: by the criterion of NR-P3, a slow figure becomes an F-series row in `docs/operations/performance-finding-register.md` (O-17); otherwise the NOTE is refuted. Recorded in the sprint log's Plan Review section with its evidence requirement (`docs/WORKFLOW.md:36`).

**N-4 — AC-C2n and AC-C2m's wave timing.** **Landed in TASK-14818-T and TASK-14825-T / TASK-14828-T (14826-T until NR-P9):** at T12 (wave 4) AC-C2n is exercised through T12's own in-force read (the repository's dated read, `published_at IS NOT NULL`), with its mutation M-10 run at CI-M4b; after T14a, PIN-2 — re-pointed by TASK-14825-T to the dated, fail-closed lookup — re-asserts that every compiled (agreement, OK version, position) resolves a published database version in force, field by field. AC-C2m's read-and-predecessor half is pinned at T12 (M-9 at CI-M4a); its pay half ("May 2026's pay uses the published version, not the draft") can only run once payroll reads dated configs, so it is written in TASK-14828-T and its mutation re-run at CI-M5.

**N-5 — two stale sentences in the refinement.** `REF:50` still says the cut order puts "the two optional pin sets go first", and `REF:902` still calls NR-23 and NR-24 open; both were superseded (`REF:833`: P-26 first; `REF:965-967`: NR-23, NR-24 ruled). **Landed here:** this plan's cut order states P-26 first, then tidy 5 and 12a, then C-10, then C-8, S0-4's served minimum last, and records NR-23 and NR-24 as ruled. The refinement is not edited (rev 5.1 READY is the record).

## Acceptance criteria → verifying test → task

*Kind: F fails on today's code · N needs the sprint's new API (Unit: one local mutation at the gate; Docker: argued, and run in a CI-M evidence run) · R regression pin. "CI" = Docker-gated or composed-stack, "CI-pending" until a checkpoint run shows it green. **Owner** = the one task whose merge closes the AC in full; where the refinement splits an AC, the earlier parts are named. Test names are contract; a renamed test is reported old → new.*

## The mutation list

*Unit mutations (M-1 … M-7, M-24 … M-26 from "final + 7", M-27 and M-28 from "final + 8", M-29 and M-30 from "final + 9", M-31 and M-32 from "final + 10", M-33 from "final + 11", M-34 … M-36 from "final + 12": M-34 and M-35 are vitest mutations run at TASK-14844's fix-up, M-36 a Docker mutation argued by reading; M-37 and M-38 from "final + 13"; and M-51 and M-52 from "final + 27") are run in an isolated worktree at the gate (`docs/AGENTS.md:177-186`); the Orchestrator reads the captured output. That worktree is detached at master, except for M-2 … M-5 (14826-T's branch-tip SHA, NR-P18), M-24 … M-26 (14813-T's branch-tip SHA, run by the 14813-T pass at its own gate, Block A) and M-27 … M-30 (14813-T's fix-up-tip SHA, the same pass's fix-up gate), **and, from "final + 10", M-24 … M-33 again (14843-T's branch-tip SHA, run by the 14843-T pass at its own gate): wave 3b rewrites the daily check, so M-24, M-26 … M-30 are re-pinned to the look-back rule (the rows below are the 3b pins; wave 3's runs and their must-trip lists are in the sprint log) and M-25 is re-run unchanged.** **From "final + 13", M-37 and M-38 run in a worktree detached at 14815-T's branch-tip SHA, by the 14815-T pass at its own gate (the pair is unmerged).** **From "final + 25", M-6 runs in a worktree detached at 14822-T's branch-tip SHA, by the 14822-T pass at its own gate (the pair is unmerged; the S148 wave-5 batch-A/B brief adjudication, A3).** **From "final + 26", M-50 (a non-Docker Regression mutation, AC-C1e's payroll-side fact) runs in a worktree detached at 14828-T's branch-tip SHA, by the 14828-T pass at its own gate (the stack is unmerged; the S148 wave-5 batch-C brief adjudication, ruling 19); it is local, in no CI mutation run.** **From "final + 27", M-7 and M-52 (AC-PIL-f) run in a worktree detached at 14831-T's branch-tip SHA, by the 14831-T pass at its own gate, and M-51 (AC-C3's round-trip pin and its size fact) in one detached at 14830-T's branch-tip SHA, by the 14830-T pass at its own gate (each pair is unmerged; the S148 wave-5 batch-D brief adjudication, rulings 15 and 20); all three are local, in no CI mutation run.** Neither wave 3 nor wave 3b has a CI mutation run. Docker mutations (M-8 … M-16, M-22, M-23, M-39 … M-49; twenty-two mutations over eighteen AC ids in five runs, CI-M2b holding M-22 and M-23 apart from M-8 (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-11); AC-C2p carrying M-15 and M-16, AC-S0-2e carrying M-8, M-22 and M-23, and AC-C2a carrying M-39 and M-40, which run apart, M-39 at CI-M4a and M-40 at CI-M4b, because both mutate the one hydration and under M-39 the shared-date fact is green; NR-P5 as counted from draft 1.4.1, M-22 and M-23 from "final + 6", M-39 and M-40 from "final + 13", M-41 (AC-C13l, CI-M4a) and M-42 (AC-PIL-h (7), CI-M4b) from "final + 14", M-43 (AC-C2r, CI-M4a) from "final + 17", M-44 (AC-C13m, CI-M4a) and M-45 (AC-C13n, CI-M4a) from "final + 18", M-46 (AC-C13o, CI-M4a) and M-47 (AC-C13p, CI-M4a) from "final + 19", M-48 (AC-C13s, CI-M4a) from "final + 24", M-49 (AC-C2u, CI-M5) from "final + 25"; addendum A1's M-17 … M-21 are listed and counted in the addendum, § 5) are argued in each pin's docstring and **run in a CI mutation evidence run** (CI-M2, CI-M2b, CI-M4a, CI-M4b, CI-M5), on a throwaway branch that never reaches master, by five rules (NR-P25, draft 1.5):*

1. **The mutation commit.** The re-spawned `test-qa` applies the run's mutations in a detached worktree. It runs Unit, DemoSeed and the non-Docker Regression facts there and records every fact that goes red. It then commits on a throwaway branch. *(NR-P31: the paired `-T` pass's own local run under the mutation, reported at its gate, is the first census; this re-run confirms it, and any difference between the two is recorded by name.)*
2. **The frozen list.** It is the pin's assertion (the row's "must trip" cell), plus every fact observed red in step 1, plus every Docker, smoke or e2e fact argued to go red, from any earlier wave. It is written in the mutation commit's message and copied to the sprint log before the push.
3. **The workflow patch.** The Orchestrator adds one commit on that branch only, "EVIDENCE ONLY — ci.yml continues past expected failures". It adds `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` to exactly three `build-and-test` steps: "Run DemoSeed tests (QUAL-096)", "Apply init.sql to the services-postgres (schema + seeds for the :5432-coupled classes)" and "Run regression tests" (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:168`, `:178`, `:187`). Nothing else changes, and the job still ends red. *Why:* the "Run unit tests" step (`:165-166`) has no continuation and those three steps have no `if:`, so an expected Unit red would skip the Regression step and the Docker pin would never be observed; the patch reaches the run because the workflow triggers on `pull_request` to master (`:4-7`), which runs the PR's version of the workflow; the `test-results` upload already runs `if: always()` (`:199-204`). Master's CI is unchanged.
4. **Reading the run.** `gh run download <run> -n test-results`. The failed set is read from the Unit, DemoSeed and Regression trx files, the `smoke-test-results` artifact (`ci.yml:266-271`), the e2e job's report (uploaded only when e2e fails, `:386-393`) and the other jobs' conclusions (NR-P31). A run with no Regression trx is not evidence and is repeated.
5. **Judging.**
   - **A pin counts as proved only when its trx failure message is the row's "must trip" assertion** (NR-P29). A pin red at an earlier assertion (a setup or save step) is not evidence for the "must trip" cell: it is recorded as what actually tripped, and the row's evidence claim is narrowed to it.
   - The pin green is a finding: the pin does not discriminate, so fix the fact and re-run. A listed **non-pin** census fact green is a census over-claim, recorded by name, with no re-run (NR-P30).
   - An unlisted red is dispositioned by its assertion message. If the mutated behaviour explains it, it is recorded by name in the sprint log's mutation table as a census miss, with no re-run. If not, it is a defect and gets a fix task.
   - Mutations batched into one run stay independent of each other's pins.

## CI checkpoints and the close sequence (Orchestrator on Opus; judgments by Fable or the labelled stand-in)

*Plain language:* Docker cannot run here, so Docker-only facts are first seen in CI. Each wave that lays a Docker-only foundation is run in CI once, on a throwaway branch, before the next wave builds on it. The sprint is then reviewed on a fixed commit **K** that has itself run green, and the close commit (docs-only on top of K) is pushed and watched.

**The mechanism (every checkpoint):** `git push origin <sha>:refs/heads/s148-ci-<name>`; open a **draft** PR to master whose first body line is "EVIDENCE ONLY — S148 checkpoint <name>; never merge"; watch with ONE background command and its completion notification (never polled); confirm `headSha == <sha>`; **all seven jobs green** (a CI-M run: the branch also carries the evidence-only `ci.yml` patch; judged against its frozen list by the mutation list's rules, NR-P25). Then close the PR unmerged, `git push origin --delete s148-ci-<name>`, `git branch -D` locally. Red where green was expected → a fix task through the merge checklist → repeat. The sprint log records each run id and only then flips the facts it showed green from "CI-pending" to "CI-verified". A watcher reaped for low memory is not restarted; the run is read by hand.

1. **CI-W0 (TASK-14800's tip; production code = `fee2a635`) — the capture.** Expected: PIN-1's two facts fail with "golden capture" and the canonical text; everything else green. **O-1:** the Orchestrator downloads `test-results` (`gh run download <run> -n test-results`), extracts the two texts verbatim, commits them as the two golden files (a generated artifact, no hand edit), and records the seeds and counts (Wave 0's report). **NOTE (Step 0b cycle 1, `c1 A-8`, `(unproven)`):** the seed is chosen from the manifest alone, so whether payroll accepts every APPROVED month at the base is first known here. If payroll refuses an APPROVED month, the refusal kind is recorded as a finding, the next qualifying seed is taken, `DemoPinSeedSelectionTests` carries the named exclusion (seed, refusal kind, run id), and CI-W0 re-runs.
2. **CI-W0b (the golden committed):** PIN-1, PIN-1b's and PIN-2's facts green; all seven jobs green. **No wave-1 merge before this.**
3. **CI-W1 (after wave 1's GATE):** AC-S0-1a … c, AC-S0-7a … e, AC-S0-9a, AC-12b-a, -b, AC-21a-a … d, AC-5a, -5b, AC-12a; the smoke suite with 14802-T's re-pointed facts; the e2e job (`skema-registration.spec.ts` under the bound); the frontend build on the regenerated client (14803); PIN-1.
4. **CI-W2 (after wave 2):** AC-S0-2b … f (AC-S0-2e as its two payroll facts and its mapping fact), h (1), i, AC-S0-5b, -5d; **addendum A1's AC-CL-1 … -20, -22 … -25, -33 … -38 (AC-S0-2j arrives as AC-CL-19) and AC-BS-1 … -5 (TASK-14842)**; the reseeded schema (the new `work_time_projection` column included); PIN-1 (on the clearing code, AC-CL-21). **CI-M2:** M-8 (frozen list: AC-S0-2e's two payroll facts, `…_Credited` and `…_ExportedUnderSls0530`, both red because the save refuses the code first, `EveryVocabularyCode_HasExactlyOneClassification`, and whatever 14811-T's local run under M-8 shows red, NR-P25; the reflection fact green; NR-P26), **plus addendum A1's M-18, M-19 and M-20 as the adjudication redefined them, and M-21 (the replayed work-time clear's `outbox_id <= @clear` guard, AC-CL-38; addendum cycle-2 ruling R6) (frozen list per 14840-T's local census)**. CI-M2 so carries five mutations: M-8 and the addendum's M-18 … M-21. **CI-M2b (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-11, on the NR-P15 precedent):** M-22 and M-23, AC-S0-2e's credit half and export half, one mutation each (wave-2 brief adjudication, 14811-T BC-4). M-22 turns red only the credit fact `…_Credited`; M-23 turns red only the export fact `…_ExportedUnderSls0530` and the mapping fact `ChildSickCallHomeMappingTests.EveryAgreement_HasAnOk26Sls0530Row`. Frozen lists per 14811-T's local census, run under each mutation alone, which must show the two reds are independent: neither mutation turns red a fact the other's frozen list relies on. *Why a run of its own:* M-8 and M-22 change the same classification row (one removes it, the other makes it `Neutral`), and under M-8 the save refuses the code (`SkemaEndpoints.cs:970`; NR-P29) before either payroll fact reaches its assertion, so in one run M-8 would hide both. Splitting AC-S0-2e's payroll fact into a credit fact and an export fact lets M-22 and M-23 share CI-M2b without either hiding the other.
5. **CI-W3 (after wave 3):** AC-S0-3a, -3c, -3f; from "final + 8" (TASK-14813 5a adjudication ruling 1), the two new Docker facts `RestLeaderVisibilityTests.LeaderRead_PreviousMonthUnsent_WithholdsTheBoundaryRest` and the Skema month-view leader fact (a leader's GET of a sent month omits an unsent neighbour's boundary row); the composed compliance probe non-503; PIN-1.
5b. **CI-W3b (after wave 3b):** AC-S0-3k, -3l, -3m; the re-pinned Docker facts (AC-S0-3a's and AC-S0-3f's, the four `RestLeaderVisibilityTests` facts whose finding moves to the 1st, `SkemaRowPreferencesMonthGetTests.BoundaryWorkTime_ExactlyThreeDays_NoiseExcluded`) and every other `RestLeaderVisibilityTests` fact green (the security consult's negatives over the two-day window); the composed compliance probe non-503 (the engine host resolves the Copenhagen zone); the frontend job (the parity vitest on schema 2); PIN-1. Wave 3b has no CI mutation run.

PIN-1. **Watch items** (the TASK-14845 Step 5a cycle-1 rulings, "final + 24"): **(R6, `(unproven)`)** the HR-on-behalf success facts must not hang, since a hang would be the lock interaction neither lens could rule out (the reopen hook's worklist upsert; repository methods neither lens read); **(R7)** all seven `OrganisationDriftGuardTests` facts execute and pass (two of seven ran locally, the count unexplained); **(R8, `(unproven)`)** Choreography B, tier 1's blocking point, stays green on repeated runs under background activity. **CI-M4a:** M-9 (read half) + M-11 + M-15 (read half: AC-C2p (1), (2), (4)) + M-39 (AC-C2a's split fact, both arms; ## The Ponytail trial — what each task records (M1–M6)

*The register's counting rules apply (`docs/operations/model-routing-register.md:73-102`): a chain is one lens on one scope; cycle 1 is its first review; a repeated finding counts once; adjudications, Constraint Validator files and security consults are not reviews; a number the records cannot supply is "not reconstructable", never estimated.*

- **Every task with a 5a row** records in its sprint-log entry: **M1** the cycles of its internal chain and, where marked, its Codex chain (fix-up re-reviews included); **M2** each chain's cycle-1 B/W/N; **M4** every `(unproven)` NOTE either lens raised, with its evidence requirement and its disposition (proved / refuted / open; "followed up" when the Orchestrator ordered the evidence in-sprint); **M5** any "Not built" item the Orchestrator overruled at merge, and any review finding of missing machinery, each traced (counted only when it traces to the SMALLEST COMPLETE CHANGE block, not to the brief or the refinement — the per-task notes' never-extras column is the reference); **M6** each `shortcut(pending)` registered at merge (the likely ones are listed per task; an unlisted one is reported too) and any check-5 false positive.
- **The sprint** records: **M1** the Step-7a chains per lens; **M3** post-close defects (at C-5 and after); CI runs to green, descriptive (every run the sprint log lists by id; the CI-M runs are expected-red evidence and counted separately, as S144's deliberate-red runs were); **the spawn reconciliation** — "log ALLOW N / transcripts M", the BLOCKs separately, each gap explained (FAIL-009's 60 s limit, first test).
- **Step 0b** (this plan's review) and the refinement chains are recorded descriptively, not as M1 cells (as the sprint log did for the refinement, `SPRINT-148.md:161-162`).
- **Already known for M4:** seven `(unproven)` NOTEs enter the sprint open or pending (the seventh, from Step 0b cycle 5: Fable c5 N-1, if M-8 is narrowed to the save gate, the "exported under `SLS_0530`" half has no red evidence from M-8; open, settled at CI-M2: the sprint log's mutation table says so in words, or 14811-T proposes a second mutation that reclassifies the code as non-credit and so passes the save; **"final + 6": settled by evidence, not words** (wave-2 brief adjudication, 14811-T BC-4): a reclassified code keeps the export half green, because the engine emits the line whatever the class, so two mutations are run, M-22 for the credit half and M-23 for the export half, at CI-M2b, apart from M-8, on a payroll fact split in two (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-11)) — R-19's (settled by AC-21a-d in TASK-14806-T), the refinement's A-10 member omission (open; evidence: a caller or serializer that omits a config member; **"final + 26": proven for the merge function and fixed in TASK-14826, QUAL-190, AC-C1p; its serializer branch ruling 22's local run with ruling 19's control arm; disposition recorded at O-17**, the S148 wave-5 batch-C brief adjudication, ruling 1), N-3 (settled at CI-W5 by the timing pair), two from Step 0b cycle 1: `c1 A-8` (PIN-1's seeds accepted by payroll at the base; settled at CI-W0) and NR-P14's real-clock publish tests (settled by TASK-14818-T's census), and one from cycle 3: Fable c3 N-1 (no mutation observes the override version row's `recorded_at` stamp, the override twin of M-16; open, settled by inspection at TASK-14818's 5a, its report quoting the version INSERT's column list). Three more are recorded in the Plan Review section with their dispositions, not open: cycle 1's NR-P11 `NOW()` inversion, **proved** in cycle 2 by Codex's concrete case and made a requirement by NR-P16, with its own N fact, AC-C2p (4), proved by M-16 (NR-P24); cycle 2's mutation-masking NOTE (Codex's NOTE ≈ the internal lens's N-5: M-15 could hide M-13 in CI-M5), **closed by condition** (NR-P22: AC-C3a's changed publish dated later than the version it replaces); and cycle 3's Fable c3 N-2 (M-10's frozen list vs AC-C2m's fixture: if AC-C2m's read fact relied on a seeded published config, M-10 would turn it red in CI-M4b), **closed by condition** (TASK-14818-T: AC-C2m's read fact publishes its own version, and M-10's census names every fact that reads a seeded config; NR-P25's frozen-list rule).

1. **The task mapping.** 28 refinement tasks → 40 dispatch ids + 24 paired passes (the mapping line under the ledger; the T14b stack's two test passes, NR-P9); the refinement's merge orders kept; the shared-files table against every task's authorized files.
2. **Coverage.** Every one of the 111 refinement AC ids, and the plan-added AC-C13j, AC-C13k, AC-C2o and AC-C2p (115 in all; 116 from "final + 6", with AC-S0-7g; 117 from "final + 7", with AC-S0-3f: 50 stage 0, 67 config stage, 85 owned by a paired `-T` pass), has exactly one owner and a test kind; the twenty-one ACs with parts in more than one task (twenty-two from "final + 6", with AC-S0-2h); no AC is owned by a task that cannot run it (DV-6: the pay halves after T14b; NR-P9: 14826-T writes only facts its tree can run).
3. **The declared deviations DV-1 … DV-7**, the rulings NR-P1 … NR-P31.
4. **N-1 … N-5's placements**, against the code cited in each.
5. **The added test areas:** the smoke facts (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Smoke/SmokeTests.cs:108` in TASK-14828-T; `:140`, `:175` in TASK-14802-T; the `check-overtime-governance` probe, `docs/WORKFLOW.md:310`); the T11 fixture and schema-copy census (`git grep -l -i -e "INSERT INTO agreement_configs" -e "INSERT INTO position_override_configs" -e "INSERT INTO local_agreement_profiles" -- tests` → 10 files; the schema copies, `ForcedRollbackHarness.cs:229`, `:297` among them, and the override version table in every copy whose tests call an override writer, NR-P28); the catalog-row timing (`AuditProjectionParityTests.cs:12-38`).
6. **The lock's key** `(148, 1)`: no two-integer advisory key in `src` today (`git grep -n -E "advisory_(xact_)?lock(_shared)?\s*\(" -- src` → every call takes one `hashtext` key or a precomputed single key, `PayrollExportRecordRepository.cs:183`); the `pg_locks` filter (classid 148, objid 1, objsubid 2).
7. **The pin's mechanics:** the seed counted from the manifest (`DemoGenerator.cs:549`, `:588-598`); the capture through the trx artifact (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:199-204`); PIN-2 not satisfiable by the compiled fallback (BC-P8).
8. **The checkpoints:** CI-W4a, CI-W5a and the CI-M runs, with the CI-M runs' workflow patch and frozen-list rule (NR-P25); no checkpoint between the T14b stack and 14831 (`REF:786`).
9. **Block C** in every brief named in "How the waves are built" point 8, and its negative in 14834 and 14836.
10. **Drafts 1.3–1.5's fold-in:** each of NR-P7 … NR-P28 is in the body, not only in "Needs a ruling" (the places each ruling's bullet names); the cycle-1, -2 and -3 findings each have a disposition (§ "Needs a ruling", the 1.3, 1.4, 1.4.1 and 1.5 rulings blocks).

| **CI** | CI-W0 <run> · CI-W0b · CI-W1 · CI-W2 / CI-M2 · CI-W3 · CI-W3b · CI-W4a · CI-W4 / CI-M4a / CI-M4b · CI-W5a · CI-W5 / CI-M5 · CI-W6 · C-2 · close <run, headSha, result each> |
| **Measurements** | Teamoversigt before/after; publish; two lock figures; manifest size; walk cost |
| **Judgment model** | Fable 5.2 available? <yes / limited since …>; stand-in artifacts so far |
| **Open rulings** | <id · question · who> |
| **Next action** | <one line> |

## Brief contradictions (claim · what the code shows · file:line at `fee2a635`)

*Claims in the brief or the refinement about the existing code or about which task can run what; I went by the code. None changes a requirement; the requirement-level ones are reported with reasoning and drafted as ruled.*

- **BC-P1 (the brief) · "`.claude/plans/` is gitignored"** · it is not: `.gitignore:39-46` lists `.claude/refinements/`, `reviews/`, `telemetry/`, `sweeps/` and others, not `plans/`; `.claude/plans/PLAN-s147.md` and 133 other plan files are tracked (`git ls-files .claude/plans`). The practical rule still holds: a plan or brief not yet committed does not reach a worktree branched from master, so briefs are inlined, and the plan and briefs are committed at the plan commit.
- **BC-P2 (the refinement's T21a row) · "test-qa (AC-C13a … f …)"** (`REF:815`) · AC-C13a's "different norm lines before and after the 15th" needs payroll's organisation boundary (T22, wave 5) and the local profile's weekly norm reaching pay (T14b; before it "payroll still pays on compiled values", `REF:414`); AC-C13b and -c are payroll-side; AC-C13g is T22's own (`REF:817`). Applied as DV-6.
- **BC-P3 (the refinement, C-2's ACs) · AC-C2a "each part is paid under its own version", AC-C2c "/recalculate then pays the month under the new version", AC-C2m "May 2026's pay uses the published version"** · payroll reads dated configs only from T14b (`REF:414`), so these halves cannot pass at T12 or T19. Applied as DV-6 (N-4 names AC-C2m's).
- **BC-P4 (the refinement) · T21a's "data-model (the transfer event)"** (`REF:815`) **vs T11's "owns … the entity, model and event shapes"** (`REF:811`). Applied as DV-4.
- **BC-P5 (the refinement's test-churn census, `REF:256`, `:413`, `:895`) · the route's and the engine contract's test churn** · the composed-stack smoke suite, which runs only in CI's compose job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:214-252`), posts to the retiring route twice (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Smoke/SmokeTests.cs:140`, `:175`) and calls `/api/rules/evaluate` with no config (`:108`), so T1's and T14b's local gates would not see either. Added to TASK-14802-T and TASK-14828-T (TASK-14826-T until NR-P9).
- **BC-P6 (a standing principle the refinement does not name) · T14b changes the `check-overtime-governance` request** (`REF:412`) · "when a sprint adds or changes a backend→service call … it MUST add or extend a composed-stack smoke probe", and that hop is the recorded composed-unproved debt (`docs/WORKFLOW.md:310`). Added to TASK-14828-T (TASK-14826-T until NR-P9) as a never-extra.
- **BC-P7 (the refinement's T11 row) · "audit mapper and catalog row (ADR-026)"** (`REF:811`) · the catalog is `docs/operations/audit-projection-catalog.md` (Orchestrator-only), parsed at test time by a non-Docker regression fact (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Regression/PhaseE/AuditProjectionParityTests.cs:12-38`). The agent reports each row; the Orchestrator writes it in the same merge (O-10, O-11, O-14, O-20).
- **BC-P8 (the refinement's PIN-2) · "the config the dated lookup resolves … (R today)"** (`REF:753`) · at the base no dated lookup exists, and today's lookup falls back to the compiled tables on a missing row or a read failure (`ConfigResolutionService.cs:127-145`, `:151-165`), so PIN-2 through it could compare compiled with compiled and pass. T0 reads the database rows through the repositories and asserts presence; 14825-T re-points it.
- **BC-P9 (the refinement's test-churn census, `REF:895`) · the mandatory "applies from" date and the new columns** · 10 test files insert into `agreement_configs`, `position_override_configs` or `local_agreement_profiles` by SQL, and schema copies exist (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Regression/Infrastructure/TxContractTests.cs:112`, `:182`; `…/Migrations/S25VersionMigrationTests.cs:46`, `:55`; `…/Config/ProfileTestSchema.cs:74`; `…/Config/LegacyProfileSchema.cs:33`; and, named in draft 1.5 (NR-P28), `…/Outbox/ForcedRollbackHarness.cs:229`, `:297`, which 14 test files apply); none is in the census. Added to TASK-14815-T, with `position_override_config_versions` added to the copies whose tests call an override writer (`ForcedRollbackHarness.cs`, `TxContractTests.cs`; NR-P28).
- **BC-P10 (a consequence the refinement does not name) · "B-9's per-day sum changes what the two month figures show only when the configured weekly norm changes inside the month"** (`REF:893`) · Teamoversigt reads the weekly norm at the users row's live OK version (`ApprovalEndpoints.cs:1346`, `:1381-1386`) while the balance reads the month's (`BalanceEndpoints.cs:159`, S137's QUAL-147 fix). Under T14b W_d comes from the dated lookup for day d, so Teamoversigt moves to each day's OK version: a visible change whenever an agreement's OK24 and OK26 weekly norms differ for a month whose OK version is not the employee's current one. Invisible on seeded data (every seeded agreement carries 37.0 in both OK versions, `init.sql:1452-1508`), so the R pins hold. It is a version-correctness gain (the domain-correctness invariant), not a regression; drafted per the refinement's formula, with N-2's record-less days on the day's OK version too (NR-P6).
- **BC-P11 (the refinement, C-11) · "a 403 through T9's self-decision helper"** (`REF:519`) · that T9 is S147's (SEC-056), not S148's T9 (manual hours); the helper is `ApprovalSelfGuard.IsSelf` (`src/Backend/StatsTid.Backend.Api/Endpoints/Helpers/ApprovalSelfGuard.cs:49-51`). The briefs name it.
- **BC-P12 (the refinement) · two stale sentences** (`REF:50`, `:902`) · N-5; superseded by `REF:833` and `REF:965-967`.
- **BC-P13 (the refinement's PIN-1b) · "(Unit, N)"** (`REF:752`) · the canonicaliser is used by the Regression project's Docker pin, and a Unit-project fact cannot reach a Regression-project class without a cross-test-project reference; PIN-1b is placed as a non-Docker Regression fact, which still runs in the local gate.
- **BC-P14 (the refinement, S0-3) · "a source search finds callers only in two test files"** (`REF:297`) · at `fee2a635`, `git grep -n -i -e EvaluateDailyRest -e EvaluateWeeklyRest -- src tests` finds the definitions (`RestPeriodRule.cs:122`, `:142`, and their doc references `:49-50`) and callers in one test file only (`tests/StatsTid.Tests.Unit/Rules/RestPeriodNotCheckedTests.cs:129-134`). No effect on the ruling; TASK-14813-T names the one file.
- **BC-P15 (the cycle-1 adjudication, NR-P10) · "engine `Program.cs` and `RuleRegistry.cs` become 14813 → 14826 → 14830"** · true for engine `Program.cs` (14813 owns its `check-compliance` mapping); 14813 does not touch `RuleRegistry.cs`, because `check-compliance` calls `RestPeriodRule.Evaluate` directly (engine `Program.cs:132-139`) and 14813's authorized files do not list it, so its chain is 14826 → 14830. The shared-files table carries the two rows separately. No effect on the ruling.
- **BC-P16 (the cycle-2 adjudication, NR-P16) · "the publish runs at READ COMMITTED (`AgreementConfigEndpoints.cs:480`, default isolation)"** · the transaction is opened one line earlier, `await conn.BeginTransactionAsync(ct)` with no isolation level at `AgreementConfigEndpoints.cs:479` (`:480` is the `try`), at `636bc7da`; TASK-14823's row already cites the publish's transaction as `:479-483`. TASK-14818's row cites `:479`. No effect on the ruling.

**Found while drafting (not a contradiction):** the edit-person drawer offers a future date while the organisation changes (`frontend/src/hooks/useEditPerson.ts:209-219`; the picker is built for scheduling, `frontend/src/pages/admin/editPerson/EffectiveDatePicker.tsx:1-32`), and C-13 refuses a future transfer date with 400 (`REF:544`), so HR's combined save "move to institution B and go part-time from 1 November" fails as a whole. **Raised while folding in draft 1.4 (ruled in 1.4.1, above):**
- **NR-P23 — a fifth unpushed commit, `636bc7da`, which the plan did not name (Found while folding in draft 1.4, under "Brief contradictions").** *In plain words:* after the plan base `fee2a635`, the Orchestrator committed `636bc7da`, which moved the review floor to Fable 5.2 in the agent definitions, the close guard and its test harness, and `docs/WORKFLOW.md`. No product code changed, so no ledger row, AC or count moves. But the plan's record of what S148's close pushes and what Step 7a must look at named only `4375bafc`, `b5ac67ad`, `fee2a635`, and the eleven lines `636bc7da` added to `docs/WORKFLOW.md` moved the two header citations of the Orchestrator-seat table. *Proposal (a factual update, no fork):* name `636bc7da` in the Predecessor, Plan base and Base-commit rows, C-4's push note and Step 7a's carried item (1), the latter saying it moved the close guard's floor (the gate S148's own close passes); re-point the two citations (`docs/WORKFLOW.md:489-500` → `:500-511`, `:494-500` → `:505-511`), keeping the `fee2a635` numbers beside them. *Alternative:* leave the header as of `fee2a635` and let the sprint log carry the fifth commit; cheaper, but C-4's push note and Step 7a's carried list would be incomplete. **Lean: the proposal.** Drafted as the lean in draft 1.4; ruled the lean in 1.4.1.
- **NR-P24 — AC-C2p (4)'s kind: F as ruled, or N with its own mutation.** *In plain words:* the new fact (4) guards one behaviour, the publish stamp taken after serialisation; its first assertion ("X's `published_at` is later than Y's") is the only one that `NOW()` turns red. NR-P16 ruled it F (CI), argued: red today because of `NOW()` at `AgreementConfigRepository.cs:449`. But the fact is written at repository level against the dated publish and the dated in-force read that TASK-14818 creates, and Block A's own rule makes such a fact N ("names a type or member this sprint creates … prove RED by ONE named mutation"), as the plan already labels its siblings AC-C2p (1), (2), AC-C2m's read half and AC-C2o. As F, the stamp assertion's red is argued only and never observed; M-15 turns (4) red through its second assertion (the inverted read), not through the stamp. *Concrete case:* the fixture must make A's transaction start before B commits ("its first statement run"); a fixture where A's transaction effectively starts later would pass with `NOW()` still in place, and nothing would show it. *Options:* (a) as ruled: F (CI), argued, no new mutation; (b) N, with a ninth Docker mutation, M-16 "the activating UPDATE stamps `published_at = NOW()`", tripping the stamp assertion, run in CI-M4b beside M-10 and M-14 (neither touches a fact that publishes its own dated drafts on Monday 9 November 2026 and reads on or after that day), kept apart from M-15, which also turns (4) red; still four runs. *Trade-off:* (a) costs nothing and matches how the plan treats its other Docker concurrency facts written over HTTP (argued F); (b) costs one mutation in an existing run, follows Block A's N rule, and actually observes that the fixture discriminates. **Lean (b).** Ruled (b) in draft 1.4.1: nine Docker mutations in four runs.
